#2833. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2832. Alex Steen grateful to return to FSU men's basketball ahead of FAMU opener

Alex Steen couldn't help but crack a smile.

The 6-foot-9 forward for Florida State men's basketball took the court for the first time in the program's exhibition win over LSU on Saturday, Oct. 24, a moment he thought he'd never have again. Steen was one of many class of 2022 athletes who sued the NCAA for an additional year of eligibility after the implementation of the "five in five" eligibility model.

He was granted that fifth year by Leon County Judge David Frank after a lengthy legal battle in a different court than Steen was used to.

"I definitely learned some more about court and law in the past two months. I'll say that. I understand a little more than I used to, but it was definitely a little stressful," Steen said. "I just kind of showed up and trusted in the process."

While the ever-focused and high-energy Steen was focused on what he could do against the Tigers, he allowed himself a moment to appreciate a welcome reception from the crowd at Donald L. Tucker Civic Center.

"It was great, honestly. I definitely heard it a little bit, and I cracked a little smile for sure. You know five months ago it was farewells and stuff. So to have a second opportunity to play here again, obviously I'm beyond grateful for that opportunity."

The five-in-five model retroactively went into effect, starting with the class of 2023 athletes, and gives them the ability to participate in five seasons of college athletics in five years.

The ruling meant Steen and other members of the class of 2022 were the lone group of athletes to not have a minimum of five years of eligibility in college in the last decade, with athletes impacted by the COVID-19 pandemic granted at least one additional year to compete, something that ended when athletes in the 2022 class enrolled in college.

That prompted court cases across the country, with Steen one of dozens of athletes seeking an additional year. He initially was set to start his pro career in Serbia before committing to finding a way back to the Seminoles program entering year two under Luke Loucks.

Months of legal work went into his eligibility case, which was initially denied before Judge Frank heard Steen's appeal and granted him a fifth year on Oct. 19. Steen was around the program during the legal process, and said it helped him remain focused.

"A lot of ups and downs for sure. Especially after the first hearing got denied and just kind of sticking with it. Obviously, it's kind of tough because you're here, but you don't know for sure. Am I going to be able to play? Am I not? So it was great having the coaches and the guys. He [Loucks] was like, 'Just focus on the court, you're good, we're going to figure it out, we're going to do everything we can," Steen said. "So it was super good to have them behind me"

Steen was an influential figure for Loucks in his first season as head coach of the program, and he averaged 6.8 points and 5.8 rebounds per game.

His addition bolsters an already strong Seminoles front-court, anchored by standout freshman Marcis Ponder alongside Colorado transfer Sebastian Rancik and Wake Forest transfer Cooper Schiweger.

It will allow Steen to take a different role in the program and offer him a chance to put more energy into shorter stints on the court, instead of being the lone big man like he was last season.

"I always try and play with full-out energy, but it is definitely tougher when you're kind of the only big guy out there. Now it's definitely, I know I'm going to go as hard as I can and be able to come out, get a quick one [rest], and keep that rotation going. So 100%. It's awesome having those guys."

While he's yet to play in his first official game for the program since his return, he will suit up for the Seminoles against FAMU on Monday, Nov. 2 in Tampa. While he said he's happy to be focused on playing basketball again and not legal battles, he said he'd allow himself another moment to soak up the opportunity he never thought he'd have.

"Definitely grateful, but you know I'll take a second and then when the ref blows the whistle I'll lock back in," Steen said. "Obviously super, super cool. Super grateful."

#2830. Hawks rookie Kingston Flemings suspended one game for elbowing Pelicans Bryce McGowens

Atlanta Hawks rookie Kingston Flemings threw a cheap-shot elbow to the face of the Pelicans' Bryce McGowens late in the Pelicans' win Saturday night and will serve a one-game suspension without pay, the league announced.

The play came after (not during) a Flemings drive to the rim against McGowens, where McGowens blocked Flemings' layup attempt, leading to a Pelicans transition bucket the other way. Something largely missed by the broadcasts was Flemings elbowing McGowen in the face as they ran back up the court — that got him a technical and ejection.

Flemings, the No. 8 pick in last June's draft (and the Hawks hope to be the point guard of the future), is averaging 7.1 points and 4.6 assists a game off the bench for Atlanta, and dropped 23 on Miami in a game last week.

#2829. VOTE: 12 nominees in Oct. 26-31 SW FL Girls Athlete of the Week poll

Each week during the school year, The News-Press and the Naples Daily News select high school girls athletes who stood out the week before based on a combination of coach reports sent to sports@news-press.com and sports@naplesnews.com and school nominations sent over email by noon Sunday. Those athletes are put in a poll each Monday on news-press.com and naplesnews.com, and you, our readers, vote on who you think did it best.

The winner of the Southwest Florida Girls Athlete of the Week poll will receive a T-shirt provided by BSN Sports and a tote bag provided by The News-Press and Naples Daily News. Voting ends at 10 a.m. Thursday each week. The winner will be announced on social media.

The previous SWFL Girls Athlete of the Week winner was Cape Coral High swimmer Taylor Tedhams.

Here are the Southwest Florida Girls Athlete of the Week nominees for Oct. 26-31:

Caine shot a 2-over 74 to finish third at the District 1A-12 tournament.

Freshwater shot a 3-over 75 to take low medalist honors and help lead the Cougars to the District 2A-12 championship.

Gencoguz shot an 85 to take low medalist honors by 10 strokes at the District 3A-12 tournament.

Urrea birdied three of her final four holes to shoot an even-par 72 and earn co-medalist honors as the Seahawks won the District 1A-12 tournament.

Bossert won the 100 backstroke (57.42) at the District 2A-9 championship at Barron Collier.

Burke won the 100 free (56.61) and 200 free (1:49.41) and was part of first-place finishes in the 200 free relay and 400 free relay to help lead the Cougars to a District 2A-9 championship.

Chappell won the 200 free (2:12.26) and was part of a first-place finish in the 400 free relay to help lead ECS to the District 1A-9 championship.

Kirby won the 500 free (25.20) at the District 2A-9 championship at Barron Collier.

Maltz won the 200 IM (2:07:29) and 100 butterfly (56.67) and was part of first-place finishes in the 200 medley relay and 400 free relay to help lead the Cougars to a District 2A-9 championship.

Seluk won the 500 free (5:06.29) at the District 2A-9 championship at Barron Collier.

Villaflor won the 50 free (25.27) and was part of a first-place finish in the 400 free relay to help lead ECS to the District 1A-9 championship.

#2828. Garrett Wilson: We have to give our fans a reason to come out

If someone flipped on Sunday's game between the Raiders and the Jets in the fourth quarter, they would have been forgiven for wondering if the Raiders were the home team at MetLife Stadium.

Raiders fans were loud enough in big moments of the game to contribute to a Jets false start and the interception by Raiders safety Jeremy Chinn to seal the 38-33 win was greeted with lusty cheers by those same fans. The loss dropped the Jets to 2-6 on the season and wide receiver Garrett Wilson said after the game that it is up to the Jets to do more to convince their own fans to make that kind of an impact.

"They had some fans in the building," Wilson said, via SNY. "We gotta handle it. . . . We got to give our fans a reason to come out. I get it. As a team we got to operate better, regardless of the noise."

The Jets are on their way to a 16th straight season without a trip to the playoffs and that run of futility makes it more surprising that they still have enough supporters turning out on Sundays to make it a surprise when the visiting side dominates the stands.

#2826. Knicks Week Preview: November 1-7, 2026

The Knicks (2-4) head into the first full week of November with a mixed slate that starts soft and gets progressively harder. Two home dates against basement-dwellers offer a chance to build momentum and pad the win column, but the road finale against an Eastern Conference rival looms as the team's next measuring stick. How New York fares will say a lot about where this group stands after a choppy 2-4 start. Or not. The season's still young.

The week opens the week with a home date against a struggling Nets squad that finally notched a win on Sunday (98-90 at Indiana) after opening 0-5. Brookly has been one of the conference's weakest teams so far, ranking near the bottom in most major categories (except pace; these youngins are quick!). They allow a high volume of points, but the addition of familiar face Julius Randle and some young pieces has produced occasional competetive stretches, including a close overtime loss to Boston.

In this, their first meeting of the season, the Knicks should control the glass at Madison Square Garden against a spunky group still chasing consistency at both ends. The home crowd, hungry for winning basketball, and Jalen Brunson's midrange game should set the tone early.

A quick turnaround brings the Bulls back to Madison Square Garden. "The Bulls again?" you ask. Yessir, our heroes already handled the Chi-Guys 133-127 on the road last week, thanks to Brunson's 38 points that helped to flip a 19-point deficit. The Bulls are 1-5 and have lost four straight, struggling defensively while leaning on newcomer Caleb Wilson, Josh Giddey and Norman Powell for production.

As we've seen, Chicago's youth and athleticism can create transition problems and second-chance opportunities, but their defensive rating has been poor and the sloppiness of youth continues to surrender easy looks. The Knicks should impose their physical side again and not let Chicago jump so far ahead in their rematch. A 19-point comeback might not be so easily attainable this time around.

The week closes with a road test against one of the east's top teams. The Cavaliers sit at 4-2 and have been scoring efficiently (top-tier offensive numbers) behind Donovan Mitchell, Evan Mobley, and James Harden. Their defense hasn't caught up yet, however. As of this writing, they're 20th in the league for points allowed and 17th for defensive rating.

Cleveland's length and ability to switch or protect the rim will challenge the Knicks' half-court sets. You'll recall that New York is a little thin at the center position. James Wiseman has played just three of six games thus far, and has sat for two straight. Against the twin towers of Jarrett Allen and Mobley, Knicks coach Mike Brown might find it necessary to deploy Wiseman for a few minutes. The Rocket Arena crowd could be a factor, too. This is the toughest of the three games as both teams try to establish themselves in a competitive Eastern Conference. Cleveland is still smarting from from the Knicks sweeping them in the Eastern Conference Finals, so don't think they haven't circled this on the calendar. Everyone wants a swing at the champs this season.

#2825. Compliance Solutions named presenting sponsor for Austin event

Everything is bigger in Texas, including a PGA Tour event with a presenting sponsor.

Just two weeks before the inaugural week of the Austin Championship gets underway, as Golfweek previously reported, the PGA Tour on Monday announced Compliance Solutions, Inc., a leader in comprehensive client-centered compliance, accounting, tax, and business advisory services, as presenting sponsor of the 2026 Austin Championship, which will debut the week of Nov. 9-15, at Omni Barton Creek Resort & Spa's Fazio Canyons Course in Austin.

More: Q&A: Austin Championship tournament director on Good Good fallout, PGA Tour's return

The tournament launched as the Good Good Championship, but Good Good stepped away from title sponsorship after backlash over a promotional video produced with then-partner Callaway. The advertisement, which promoted a new Callaway driver, drew criticism for depicting a man shoving a woman to the ground.

"The PGA Tour is pleased to welcome Compliance Solutions as presenting sponsor of the inaugural Austin Championship," said Dan Glod, PGA Tour Executive Vice President, Corporate Partnerships. "We are proud to have their commitment and support as we prepare to bring this new event online."

Compliance Solutions has served as title sponsor of the Compliance Solutions Championship, a Korn Ferry Tour event, since 2023, and has been the presenting sponsor of the Stifel Charity Classic on PGA Tour Champions since 2025. This year, the company also sponsored the Route 66 Cup, a season-long competition celebrating the 100th anniversary of the historic route, with a $100,000 bonus pool that concluded at its tournament at the Patriot Golf Club in Owasso, Oklahoma. Also introduced this year was the Sprint to St. Louis presented by Compliance Solutions, a six-event, points-based competition spanning the final five events of the PGA Tour Champions regular season and the first Charles Schwab Cup Playoffs event at the Stifel Charity Classic.

Compliance Solutions is also an enthusiastic supporter of Folds of Honor, helping to bring the National Anthem to golf by adding their name to Folds of Honor Friday programs at Constellation Furyk & Friends and the Stifel Charity Classic, as well as Folds of Honor Collegiate at American Dunes Golf Course in Grand Haven, Michigan.

"The PGA Tour and its tournaments do an incredible job of giving back to the communities where they operate and serve," said Compliance Solutions founder and president Mark Lammert. "We are proud to join forces with the Austin Championship to support the tournament and help elevate the event in its efforts to drive charitable support for the city of Austin and beyond, and to continue to grow the game of golf through the world-class PGA Tour competition on the Canyons course."

The Austin Championship is the seventh event out of eight on the FedEx Cup Fall schedule.

Adam Schupak joined Golfweek in 2006 and covers the PGA Tour, the business of golf, and enjoys contributing to golf travel and architecture.
